  • Sump pump and easement status were treated as unknown unless a listing explicitly mentioned a sump pump or other issue; absence of a consumer-listing mention was not treated as verified clearance.
  • Sump pump and easement statuses are generally unknown unless a listing explicitly disclosed a sump pump or other issue. I did not infer clear status from silence.
  • Sump pump and easement statuses are left as unknown unless listing text affirmatively disclosed a problem or clear condition; absence of mention was not treated as proof.
  • Sump pump and easement statuses are marked unknown unless explicitly disclosed or contradicted by listing data. Consumer listings rarely prove absence of sump pumps or easements.
  • Sump pump and easement statuses are unknown unless the listing affirmatively reported a problem. For example, 16 Central St had both sump pump and easements disclosed and therefore was excluded because it also lacked verified garage.
  • Zillow and Realtor sometimes reported different living-area figures for the same property, especially 64 Haverhill St; the more complete current listing metadata was used, and discrepancies should be verified directly against MLS disclosures.
  • "UNKNOWN" for sump/easement does not mean the house is clear. Verify seller disclosures, deed/plot plan, and MLS details before relying on it.
  • Road-noise judgments from web research are screening tools only. Driving the area at rush hour is still worthwhile.
